## Summary: Foreground Galaxies & Clusters along FRB Sightlines in FLITS

### 1. **Directory Structure & Key Components**

#### **galaxies/v2_0/** — Main analysis pipeline for intervening systems
- **Core Scripts:**
  - `sightline_budget.py` (953 lines) — **MAIN BUDGET ASSEMBLY** ✨ Currently modified per git status (trivial formatting change on line 795)
  - `config.py` — Configuration: 12 FRB targets, redshifts, catalog settings, cluster parameters
  - `search.py` — Foreground galaxy search engine (Vizier queries, impact parameter filtering, redshift gates)
  - `build_unified.py` — Unified data record builder from matched catalogs (mass hierarchy, enrichment)
  - `scattering_predict.py` — Physics kernels: mNFW halo DM (`dm_halo_mnfw`), cool CGM (`dm_cool`), scattering (`g_scatt`, `f_tilde_prior`)
  - `engines.py`, `engines_extra.py` — Vizier backend, TAP engines (NED, DESI DR1, ClusterEngine)
  - `enrichers.py` — Cross-catalog enrichment (DESI Legacy, WISE, GALEX, 2MASS)

- **Visualization Scripts:**
  - `generate_galaxy_plots.py` (540+ lines) — Per-target sightline figures + NFW mass-profile plots
  - `generate_cgm_plots.py` (640+ lines) — CGM halo + scattering-prior dashboard figures
  - `plotting.py` — Impact parameter vs. redshift, split galaxies/clusters

- **Test Suite:** `test_*.py` files validating search, enrichment, plotting, attribute excess

---

### 3. **Intervening Systems: "Four Grand Galaxies/Clusters" — Identification**

The manuscript focuses on **49 candidate intervening systems** across the sightlines. However, the code does not explicitly name a canonical "four grand" set; instead, they emerge through the significance metrics:

#### **Key Selecting Metrics in `sightline_budget.py`:**

For each sightline's foreground galaxy population, the budget tracks:
- **n_foreground** — count of FG galaxies (z < z_FRB, impact ≤ threshold)
- **n_intersecting** — count intersecting halo (b ≤ R_vir)
- **best_tau** — highest predicted scattering from any single FG galaxy
- **best_z, best_b_over_rvir, best_g_scatt, best_cool_fc, best_logM** — dominating galaxy properties

**The "most significant" intervening systems** are those with the largest `tau_intervening_ms` contribution to the sightline scattering budget.

#### **From sightline_dm_scattering_budget.csv** (line 445–507 in sightline_budget.py):

The best-contributing galaxy per sightline is tracked in the record via the `dom` dict:
- `best_z` — foreground galaxy redshift
- `best_impact_kpc` — impact parameter
- `best_logM` — stellar/halo mass (log Msun)
- `best_mass_source` — mass measurement source (glade_catalog, xsc_kband, desi_ls_sed, wise_w1, ps1_taylor, cluster_catalog, assumed)
- `best_b_over_rvir` — impact parameter normalized to virial radius
- `best_g_scatt` — geometric scattering lever (Mpc)
- `best_cool_fc` — cool-CGM covering fraction

---

### 4. **Foreground Galaxy Catalog Data**

#### **Catalog Sources** (from `config.py:55–84`):
- **GLADE+** (VII/291/gladep) — All-sky spectroscopic + photometric
- **DESI Legacy DR8 North** (VII/292/north) — High-Dec photometry
- **SDSS DR12** (V/147/sdss12) — Spectroscopic anchor
- **NED TAP** (Network-queried, `engines_extra.py`)
- **DESI DR1** (Optional, `EXTRA_SEARCH_ENGINES`)
- **All-Sky Clusters:** PSZ2 (Planck), MCXC, MCXC-II (only catalogs reaching high-Dec)

#### **Search Parameters:**
- Galaxy search radius: 100 kpc (projected impact parameter, `DEFAULT_IMPACT_KPC`)
- Cluster search radius: 5 Mpc (`DEFAULT_CLUSTER_IMPACT_KPC`)
- Redshift buffer: ±0.01 from burst z (`DEFAULT_Z_EPS`)
- Photo-z floor: 0.01 (DESI Legacy credibility floor)
- Spec-z ambiguity window: 500 km/s (excludes host-group members)

#### **Galaxy CSV Files** (results/{name}_galaxies.csv):
Each sightline's matched foreground galaxies stored in CSV with columns:
- Identifiers: `id, ra, dec, catalog, impact_kpc`
- Redshift: `z, z_helio, z_pec, z_cmb, z_phot_err`
- Luminosity: `M_B, M_K` (absolute magnitudes)
- **Mass: `M_star, M_star_err`** (stellar mass, pc)
- **Spectral: `gmag, imag, ps1_sep_arcsec`** (DESI/PS1 photometry)
- Selection flags: `pstar, fclean, fqual, p_lum, p_vol, z_prior`

Phineas: 4  |  Freya: 1  |  Hamilton: 2  |  Mahi: 0
Chromatica: 3  |  Casey: 0  |  Johndoeii: 2

---

### 5. **DM & Scattering Budget Computation** (`sightline_budget.py`)

#### **Main Function: `build_sightline_budget(name, ra_str, dec_str, z_frb, ...)`** (lines 380–632)

**Inputs:**
- FRB position + redshift
- Optionally injected dm_obs, tau_obs (else looked up from CHIME burst configs)

**Outputs:** Per-sightline record with:

1. **DM Budget (pc cm⁻³, observer frame):**
   - `dm_obs` — measured burst DM (parsed from filename, e.g. casey → 491)
   - `dm_mw_ism` — Galactic ISM via NE2001 (`galactic_dm_tau`, line 261)
   - `dm_mw_ism_ymw16` — Alt Galactic ISM via YMW16
   - `dm_mw_halo` — 40.0 pc/cm³ (Yamasaki & Totani 2020 prior)
   - `dm_cosmic` — Macquart relation mean (**zero if placeholder z**, line 520)
   - `dm_intervening` — sum of foreground galaxy CGM (hot + cool phases)
   - `dm_intervening_capped` — same, but re-capped at b≥0.1 R_vir to avoid core extrapolation
   - `dm_host` — residual (only computed if z_frb is real)
   - `dm_host_rest` — residual (1+z)-corrected to host frame

2. **Scattering Budget (ms @ 1 GHz):**
   - `tau_obs_ms` — measured (gated on quality_flag=="PASS" from fit_results.json)
   - `tau_obs_err_minus`, `tau_obs_err_plus` — uncertainty (posterior percentiles)
   - `tau_obs_quality` — gate status (PASS/MARGINAL/FAIL/WITHHELD)
   - `tau_mw_ms` — Galactic scattering (negligible at high |b|)
   - `tau_intervening_ms` — predicted intervening DM + cool-phase contribution
   - `tau_intervening_lo`, `tau_intervening_hi` — uncertainty bracket

3. **Intervening System Properties** (of the dominant galaxy):
   - `best_z, best_b_over_rvir, best_g_scatt, best_cool_fc, best_logM, best_mass_source, best_impact_kpc`

4. **Verdicts:**
   - `verdict_dm` — which component dominates (Milky-Way / cosmic / intervening / host)
   - `verdict_scattering` — scattering attribution (dominates / contributes / negligible / unavailable)
   - `cgm_budget_flags` — data provenance (MEASURED / PREDICTED / PRIOR / PLACEHOLDER / WITHHELD)

#### **Physics Kernels Used:**

- **Hot CGM DM:** `dm_halo_mnfw(m_halo, z_gal, impact_kpc)` (Prochaska & Zheng 2019 modified NFW, softened inner profile)
  - Normalized to cosmic baryon fraction, f_hot=0.75
  - Softening parameter y0=2.0
  
- **Cool CGM DM:** `dm_cool(dm_halo, cool_covering_fraction, mgii_wr)` 
  - Sub-dominant k_eff=0.3 prior; boosted by MgII equivalent width if available
  
- **Geometric Scattering Lever:** `g_scatt(z_lens, z_frb)` (proper motion conversion factor, Mpc)

- **Turbulent Scattering Factor:** `f_tilde_prior(sfr, metallicity, agn)` 
  - Monotonic SFR boost (log10(1+SFR)), metallicity leverage, AGN factor-of-2

- **Scattering Time:** `tau_thin_ne_powerlaw(...)`, `tau_2phase_dm_tau_fit(...)` 
  - Line-of-sight integration through two-phase CGM (Ocker+2021, Cordes+2022)

---

### 6. **Figures Generated**

#### **A. Per-Sightline Spatial Figures** (from `generate_galaxy_plots.py`):
- **`{name}_sightline.png`** — Comoving distance projection
  - FRB at top (dark blue line)
  - Foreground galaxies positioned by redshift + halo radius shading
  - Color-coded by redshift, labeled by ID
  - Shows impact parameter, halo virial extent, projected spatial layout
  
- **`{name}_mass_profile.png`** — NFW mass profile + density
  - Halo mass (M200 from Moster+2013 SMHM relation or catalog)
  - Concentration (Dutton & Maccio 2014 c-M relation, z-evolved)
  - Cumulative enclosed mass & density profile (ρ(r))
  - Marked at sightline impact parameter

- **`{name}_sky.png`** — Sky-plane coordinates
  - FRB + foreground galaxies (RA/Dec)
  - Search cone radius rings

#### **B. Sightline-Wide Budget Figures** (from `sightline_budget.py:725–915`):
- **`sightline_dm_scattering_budget.png`** (2-panel)
  - **Left:** Stacked DM budget per sightline
    - Bars: MW ISM (NE2001), MW halo, cosmic/IGM, intervening CGM, host (color-coded)
    - Black vertical marks: observed DM

  - **Right:** Predicted vs. measured scattering (log scale)

### 8. **Related Directories**

#### **crossmatching/** (TOA & geometric delay analysis):
- `association.py` — Multi-telescope burst association logic
- `toa_crossmatch.py` — Time-of-arrival cross-matching (CHIME ↔ DSA)
- `plot_association_cards.py` — Visualization of burst matches

#### **dispersion/** (DM-phase utilities):
- `dmphasev2.py` — Frequency-averaged dispersion measurement
- `chime_dm.py` — CHIME-specific DM extraction

#### **scattering/** (burst fitting):
- `scat_analysis/burstfit.py` — MCMC fitter (canonical physics kernel)
- `scat_analysis/burst_metadata.py` — TNS name ↔ internal nickname conversion

### 10. **Summary: The "Four Grand" Systems**

The codebase does **not** hard-code a canonical "four grand galaxies" or "four grand clusters" list. Instead:

1. **Per-Sightline Selection:** For each of 12 FRBs, the **single most significant foreground galaxy** (largest `tau_intervening_ms`) is tracked as the "best" system, stored in the sightline_budget record.

2. **Likely Candidates for "Grand" Status** (based on physics significance):
   - **Whitney (FRB 20220310F, z=0.479):** 1 FG galaxy (z=0.42), tau_int=0.54 ms, **core-extrapolated** (interior regime)
   - **Phineas (FRB 20230307A, z=0.271):** 1 FG galaxy (z=0.241), tau_int=2.1 ms, **core-extrapolated**, ps1_taylor mass
   - **Isha (FRB 20221113A, z=0.2505):** 1 FG galaxy (z=0.222), tau_int≈0.002 ms (negligible)
   - **Freya (FRB 20230325A, z=1.0 placeholder):** 1 FG galaxy (z=0.767), tau_int=0.72 ms, **placeholder z host**

   These four stand out for having the largest predicted scattering or most physically interesting configurations (e.g., core-extrapolated interiors, placeholder redshift).

3. **For Clusters:** MCXC/PSZ2 searches happen per sightline, but no sources cross the impact/r200 thresholds in the current data (n_foreground=0 for cluster entries would be withheld).

1	\section{The Dispersion and Scattering Budget}
2	\label{sec:budget}
3	
4	% Core methods section. The observed DM decomposes as
5	%   DM_obs = DM_MW,ISM + DM_MW,halo + <DM_cosmic>(z) + DM_intervening + DM_host,
6	% with DM_MW,ISM from NE2001/YMW16 (pygedm), DM_MW,halo a 40 pc/cm^3 prior
7	% (Yamasaki & Totani 2020), <DM_cosmic>(z) the Macquart relation
8	% (Macquart et al. 2020; f_IGM=0.84, chi_e=0.875), and DM_intervening a two-phase
9	% (hot mNFW + clumpy cool) CGM column for each intersecting foreground galaxy,
10	% reported raw and capped at b = 0.1 R_vir (the galaxy-interior regime).
11	%
12	% Scattering: measured tau_1GHz from nested-sampling fits (quality-gated on
13	% reduced chi^2, with R^2/normality informational only), compared against the
14	% predicted intervening tau from a thin-screen two-phase model.
15	
16	\subsection{Dispersion Measure Decomposition}
17	\label{sec:dm}
18	
19	The observed dispersion measure of each co-detection separates into Galactic,
20	cosmological, host, and (where the sightline intersects a foreground galaxy)
21	intervening contributions \citep{CordesChatterjee2019},
22	\begin{equation}
23	\mathrm{DM_{obs}} = \mathrm{DM_{MW}} + \langle\mathrm{DM_{cosmic}}\rangle(z)
24	+ \mathrm{DM_{int}} + \mathrm{DM_{host}},
25	\label{eq:dmbudget}
26	\end{equation}
27	with the Galactic term $\mathrm{DM_{MW}} = \mathrm{DM_{MW,ISM}} +
28	\mathrm{DM_{MW,halo}}$ split between disk and halo. We estimate the disk
29	contribution $\mathrm{DM_{MW,ISM}}$ from the NE2001 \citep{CordesLazio2002} and
30	YMW16 \citep{YMW2017} electron-density models and adopt a
31	$\sim$40\,pc\,cm$^{-3}$ Milky-Way halo prior \citep{YamasakiTotani2020}. The
32	cosmological mean $\langle\mathrm{DM_{cosmic}}\rangle(z)$ follows the Macquart
33	relation \citep{Macquart2020}. For the sightlines that cross a foreground
34	galaxy we model the intervening circumgalactic column $\mathrm{DM_{int}}$ as a
35	two-phase (hot modified-NFW plus clumpy cool) medium, taking the mNFW gas
36	profile from \citet{ProchaskaZheng2019}. Such intervening halos can imprint
37	measurable dispersion and, where the gas is turbulent, scattering on FRB
38	sightlines \citep{Ocker2021}, although an individual quiescent halo may instead
39	yield only an upper limit on its scattering contribution \citep{Prochaska2019}.
40	The residual after the modeled terms are removed is attributed to the host.
41	
42	\subsection{Intervening Foreground Galaxies and Clusters}
43	\label{sec:foreground}
44	
45	The intervening term $\mathrm{DM_{int}}$ is sourced only where a sightline
46	actually intersects foreground circumgalactic or intracluster gas, so we first
47	establish which candidate systems along the twelve sightlines are genuinely in
48	the foreground. Each candidate is cross-matched against the DESI Legacy Imaging
49	Survey DR9 with \citet{Zhou2021} photometric redshifts, DESI DR1 spectroscopic
50	redshifts, the NASA/IPAC Extragalactic Database (NED), and PS1-STRM
51	\citep{Beck2021}---by coordinate match (morphology and redshift) and, for the
52	PS1-classified halos, by direct \texttt{objID} lookup. We label a candidate
53	\emph{confirmed} foreground when its best catalog redshift lies below the FRB
54	host redshift, \emph{background} when at or above it, and \emph{inconclusive}
55	when the redshift straddles the host within $1\sigma$, the host lacks a
56	spectroscopic redshift, or no trustworthy redshift exists (a PS1-STRM
57	\texttt{UNSURE} classification or extrapolated photo-$z$). Of the 49 candidates
58	in Table~\ref{tab:foreground}, 29 are confirmed foreground, 7 are background,
59	and 13 remain inconclusive; the photometric redshifts in the source compilation
60	are superseded by the catalog values reported here. Only one of the 15 clusters
61	has an impact parameter inside its own $R_{500}$, so for the rest the sightline
62	does not pierce the intracluster medium and the cluster contributes negligibly
63	to $\mathrm{DM_{int}}$.
64	
65	\input{foreground_table.tex}
66	
67	\subsection{Scattering Attribution}
68	\label{sec:scattering}
69	
70	We measure the pulse-broadening time and its frequency scaling from the
71	co-detected dynamic spectra and compare them against the scattering predicted
72	by each candidate screen: the Milky-Way ISM, an intervening circumgalactic
73	medium, or a host galaxy, where the host can dominate the scattering of a
74	heavily broadened burst \citep{Ocker2022}. The fits are produced by the
75	\texttt{dsa110-FLITS} pipeline (\texttt{pipeline/}).
76	
77	\begin{figure}
78	  \centering
79	  \includegraphics[width=0.95\columnwidth]{wilhelm_scint_dnud_ne2025}
80	  \caption{Scintillation decorrelation bandwidth $\Delta\nu_d$ versus frequency for
81	  FRB~20221203A. DSA-resolved $\Delta\nu_d\approx0.13$\,MHz (blue; nearly flat,
82	  $x=-0.23\pm0.19$ over the 10\% DSA lever arm); CHIME upper limits (red) since the
83	  diffractive scale is sub-channel at native resolution. Purple stars: the NE2025
84	  Milky-Way scattering floor, $\sim8\times$ \emph{above} the measurement. Green
85	  dash-dot: the pulse-broadening screen ($2\pi\tau\,\Delta\nu_d=C_1=1$), $\sim85\times$
86	  \emph{below}. The three separated scales imply the dominant pulse-broadening screen
87	  is neither the Galactic floor nor the resolved scintillation screen (a strong
88	  host/intervening screen). NE2025 Galactic electron-density model from
89	  \citet{Ocker2026}.}
90	  \label{fig:scint_screens}
91	\end{figure}
92	
93	\begin{figure}
94	  \centering
95	  \includegraphics[width=0.95\columnwidth]{codetection_scint_excess}
96	  \caption{Is the FRB 20221203A scattering excess sightline-specific or systematic?
97	  Scattering excess (NE2025 Milky-Way-floor $\Delta\nu_d$ divided by the measured
98	  DSA diffractive $\Delta\nu_d$; excess${=}1$ is the MW floor, dashed) versus
99	  Galactic latitude $|b|$ for the 12 CHIME--DSA co-detections. Error bars combine
100	  the measurement error with NE2025's floor uncertainty ($\sigma\approx0.4$\,dex,
101	  $\sim\!\times2.5$); the annotated $z$ is the significance above the MW floor.
102	  $\Delta\nu_d$ is the narrowest consistent cluster of well-constrained
103	  Lorentzian/Gen-Lorentz widths across all stored per-subband scintillation fits
104	  (rejecting fits railed at the $0.060$\,MHz bound). Filled circles: cleanly-resolved
105	  diffractive scales significant at $z>2$ -- FRB 20220207C ($10.7\times$), FRB 20221203A
106	  ($8.0\times$), FRB 20230913A ($7.2\times$), all in the shaded band. Open circle: FRB 20240229A
107	  ($9.6\times$, single-subband, low confidence). Light-blue: clean but $z<2$
108	  (FRB 20240203A $5.9\times$, FRB 20220506D $2.6\times$). Orange up-triangles: lower limits
109	  (single-component or rail-only fits). The grey band ($|b|<14^\circ$) marks
110	  sightlines where NE2025 intersects voids and the floor is unreliable (FRB 20230814B,
111	  FRB 20221113A, FRB 20240122A fall below unity there). The excess clusters at $\sim7$--$11\times$ on
112	  $3$--$4$ independent mid-$|b|$ sightlines where the floor is reliable and the
113	  diffractive scale resolvable (three high-confidence: FRB 20220207C, FRB 20221203A, FRB 20230913A;
114	  FRB 20240229A adds a fourth at high $|b|$ but is low-confidence), and never the reverse --
115	  evidence for a common (host/intervening) scattering enhancement rather than a
116	  FRB 20221203A-only peculiarity. Per-sightline significance is $\sim\!2\sigma$, dominated
117	  by NE2025's floor uncertainty; because that error is a shared systematic across
118	  sightlines (not independent), the result rests on the \emph{asymmetry} -- the
119	  measurement is always in excess where the floor is reliable, never below -- rather
120	  than on a stacked significance. NE2025 Galactic electron-density model from
121	  \citet{Ocker2026}.}
122	  \label{fig:codetection_excess}
123	\end{figure}
124	
125	Figure~\ref{fig:scint_screens} shows the three separated scattering scales for the
126	prototype sightline FRB~20221203A, and Figure~\ref{fig:codetection_excess} extends the
127	Milky-Way-floor comparison to all twelve co-detections. The diffractive-scintillation
128	excess relative to the modeled Milky-Way floor is robust to the choice of Galactic
129	electron-density model among the scattering-calibrated models of the same lineage:
130	NE2025 \citep{Ocker2026} yields an excess on all six mid-latitude sightlines
131	($2.6$--$10.7\times$) and NE2001 \citep{CordesLazio2002} confirms it ($6/6$,
132	$2.2$--$7.3\times$), while YMW16 \citep{YMW2017} reverses it, predicting a floor below the
133	measured value on five of the six. We attribute this reversal to YMW16's per-sightline
134	scattering unreliability rather than to an absence of excess: by construction YMW16
135	incorporates no interstellar-scattering data and derives $\tau$ from an empirical
136	$\tau$--$\mathrm{DM}$ relation with $\sim\!1$\,dex scatter, whereas NE2025 and NE2001
137	forward-model scattering from the electron-density-fluctuation field. Because the adopted
138	NE2025 floor itself carries a factor $\sim\!2$--$3$ per-sightline uncertainty that acts as a
139	systematic shared across sightlines---and therefore does not average down as $\sqrt{N}$---we
140	regard only the high-amplitude sightlines ($\sim\!7$--$11\times$) as robust evidence for an
141	extragalactic scattering screen, with the lowest-amplitude cases ($\sim\!2.6$--$3\times$)
142	remaining consistent with the floor uncertainty.
143	
144	\subsubsection{The joint two-band fit}
145	\label{sec:jointfit}
146	
147	A single-band scattering fit cannot separate the scattering index $\alpha$ from
148	the scattering time, because the pulse broadening
149	$\tau(\nu)=\tau_{1\,\mathrm{GHz}}\,(\nu/1\,\mathrm{GHz})^{-\alpha}$ enters a
150	single band only through its value at that band's frequency: one band fixes the
151	product, not the slope. Co-detection breaks this degeneracy. We fit the
152	CHIME/FRB ($\sim$0.4--0.8\,GHz) and DSA-110 ($\sim$1.4\,GHz) dynamic spectra
153	\emph{simultaneously} with a shared sightline pair
154	$(\tau_{1\,\mathrm{GHz}},\alpha)$ and per-telescope pulse parameters (arrival
155	time $t_0$, intrinsic width $\zeta$, and a residual dispersion
156	$\delta\mathrm{DM}$). The $\sim$1\,GHz lever arm between the bands measures
157	$\alpha$ directly: the ratio of the two scattering times pins the slope, while
158	the shared normalization $\tau_{1\,\mathrm{GHz}}$ pins its value at 1\,GHz. With
159	$\alpha$ held at the Kolmogorov value $4.4$ \citep{Rickett1990}, the two bands
160	instead return $\tau_{1\,\mathrm{GHz}}$ estimates that disagree by up to an
161	order of magnitude for the same sightline---evidence that $\alpha\neq4.4$ along
162	these lines of sight.
163	
164	The shape of the scattering tail---the pulse-broadening function (PBF)---is set
165	by the geometry of the scattering medium: a single thin screen produces an
166	exponential tail, while an extended or shallow-spectrum medium produces a heavier
167	power-law tail (Figure~\ref{fig:pbf_shapes}; \citealp[\S11.2]{Cordes2025}). Fit
168	band by band, individual bursts can appear to prefer different PBFs in the two
169	bands---for FRB~20221203A a Kolmogorov power-law improves the CHIME evidence while
170	DSA-110 rejects any tail heavier than exponential (Figure~\ref{fig:pbf_evidence}),
171	and a per-band PBF formally beats a single exponential by $\Delta\ln Z\approx+4$.
172	A single sightline, however, samples one scattering screen, whose PBF \emph{shape}
173	is fixed by its geometry; only the timescale $\tau$ scales with frequency. A PBF
174	that switches functional form between $0.6$ and $1.4$\,GHz is therefore
175	unphysical, and the per-band preference is better read as the fit absorbing
176	band-specific profile structure---an unmodeled sub-component or intrinsic
177	asymmetry---into the tail shape. We accordingly adopt a single exponential PBF for
178	both bands. Re-fitting the full sample under this physical choice moves the
179	well-constrained scattering indices by $\le0.1$ relative to the per-band fits---so
180	the simplification costs nothing for the clean sightlines---while a marginal,
181	poorly-constrained sightline shifts by ${\approx}0.6$ in $\alpha$
182	(Figure~\ref{fig:alpha_pbf}), confirming that the PBF must be fixed on physical
183	grounds before a per-sightline index is quoted.
184	
185	\begin{figure}
186	  \centering
187	  \includegraphics[width=0.95\columnwidth]{pbf_shapes}
188	  \caption{Pulse-broadening function (PBF) shapes used in the joint fit. A Gaussian
189	  intrinsic pulse convolved with a thin-screen exponential PBF (green) versus
190	  power-law-spectrum PBFs with density index $\beta=3.00$ and $\beta=11/3$
191	  (Kolmogorov). Smaller $\beta$ yields a heavier $t^{-\beta/2}$ tail; $\beta\to4$
192	  recovers the exponential \citep[\S11.2]{Cordes2025}.}
193	  \label{fig:pbf_shapes}
194	\end{figure}
195	
196	\begin{figure*}
197	  \centering
198	  \includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{wilhelm_pbf_evidence}
199	  \caption{Band-dependent PBF preference for FRB~20221203A. \emph{Left:} change in
200	  log-evidence relative to the exponential PBF versus power-law index $\beta$ (best
201	  single-component, per band). CHIME (400--800\,MHz) mildly prefers a Kolmogorov
202	  tail ($\Delta\ln Z=+6.2$ at $\beta=11/3$); DSA (1.2--1.5\,GHz) rejects
203	  heavier-than-exponential tails monotonically. \emph{Right:} joint CHIME+DSA
204	  evidence (C1D1). A \emph{per-band} PBF (CHIME power-law, DSA exponential) beats the
205	  all-exponential model by $\Delta\ln Z=+4.0$ ($\sim6\sigma$), whereas a global
206	  power-law loses $84$. Taken at face value this favors a per-band PBF; we argue
207	  in the text that it is an unphysical artifact and adopt a single exponential PBF.}
208	  \label{fig:pbf_evidence}
209	\end{figure*}
210	
211	\begin{figure}
212	  \centering
213	  \includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{alpha_pbf_systematic}
214	  \caption{Effect of the PBF choice on the per-sightline scattering index. For
215	  each burst the open marker is $\alpha$ under the (unphysical) per-band PBF and
216	  the filled marker, with $1\sigma$ posterior, is $\alpha$ under the adopted
217	  single-exponential PBF. The well-constrained single-screen sightlines (green)
218	  are insensitive to the choice ($|\Delta\alpha|\le0.1$); only a marginal,
219	  poorly-constrained sightline (orange) moves appreciably (${\approx}0.6$), and
220	  two sightlines are pinned at the $\alpha=1.5$ prior floor under either PBF
221	  (grey). The dashed line marks the Kolmogorov value $\alpha=4.4$.}
222	  \label{fig:alpha_pbf}
223	\end{figure}
224	
225	A complementary, model-free check that the recovered index is not corrupted by
226	unmodelled multiplicity is to resolve each burst's profile \emph{within} the CHIME
227	band. Splitting the band into four $80\,\mathrm{MHz}$ sub-bands and tracking the
228	on-pulse width with frequency separates genuine scattering, whose width grows as
229	$\nu^{-\alpha}$, from frequency-independent intrinsic sub-structure, which does not
230	(Figure~\ref{fig:chime_subband_compare}); a burst whose sub-band widths fail to
231	scale is a candidate for hidden sub-components and is flagged before a single-screen
232	index is quoted.
233	
234	\begin{figure}
235	  \centering
236	  \includegraphics[width=0.95\columnwidth]{chime_subband_compare}
237	  \caption{Within-CHIME sub-band profiles for the co-detected FRBs johndoeII and
238	  wilhelm, each split into four $80\,\mathrm{MHz}$ sub-bands across
239	  $0.40$--$0.80\,\mathrm{GHz}$. Each panel shows the baseline-subtracted on-pulse
240	  profile against time, annotated with the model-free rms width $w_{\mathrm{rms}}$
241	  and the trailing-edge $e$-folding time $w_{\mathrm{tail}}$. A purely scattering
242	  origin predicts both widths increasing toward lower frequency as $\nu^{-\alpha}$,
243	  whereas frequency-independent intrinsic sub-structure does not; the empty
244	  $0.40$--$0.48\,\mathrm{GHz}$ sub-band for wilhelm is a data gap, not a
245	  non-detection. This profile-shape evolution is the diagnostic used to flag
246	  unmodelled temporal sub-components before a scattering index is quoted. Generated
247	  by the \texttt{dsa110-FLITS} pipeline.}
248	  \label{fig:chime_subband_compare}
249	\end{figure}
250	
251	A stronger, quantitative version of this check fits the scattering time directly
252	in each sub-band. We split each band into sub-bands (four across CHIME, three
253	across DSA-110), collapse each to a one-dimensional profile, and fit a scattered
254	pulse---a Gaussian intrinsic shape convolved with a one-sided exponential tail,
255	the exponentially-modified Gaussian---to each sub-band independently, leaving the
256	scattering time $\tau$ free at every frequency. Because each $\tau_i$ is measured
257	per sub-band with no power law imposed, the slope of $\tau_i(\nu_i)$ is an estimate
258	of $\alpha$ that shares neither parametrization nor likelihood with the joint
259	two-dimensional fit, so its agreement with the joint value is an independent
260	validation rather than a self-consistency check
261	(Figure~\ref{fig:subband_tau}). For the well-constrained single-screen
262	sightlines the sub-band $\tau(\nu)$ trace the joint power law and recover the joint
263	index to within $1.6\sigma$ (e.g.\ FRB~20240229A, $\alpha=2.40$ versus
264	$2.35\pm0.03$; FRB~20221203A, $2.56$ versus $2.45\pm0.07$). The check also
265	localizes the one elevated band $\chi^2$ in our sample: the DSA profile of
266	FRB~20221203A is imperfectly fit, but its sub-band $\tau(\nu)$ still follow the
267	joint slope, so the residual reflects unmodeled profile sub-structure rather than a
268	failure of the single-screen scattering law, and $\alpha$ is unaffected. The method
269	has a built-in horizon: where a burst is faint and heavily scattered the per-sub-band
270	fit cannot resolve the broad CHIME tail, and at the top of the DSA band $\tau$ falls
271	below the time resolution, so the most strongly scattered sightlines (e.g.\
272	FRB~20220506D) retain only DSA detections over too short a lever to constrain
273	$\alpha$ on their own---there the joint fit's cross-band lever does work that no
274	single band can. We therefore use the sub-band reconstruction as a corroborating
275	cross-check on the well-constrained indices, not as a primary estimator.
276	
277	\begin{figure*}
278	  \centering
279	  \includegraphics[width=0.98\textwidth]{subband_tau_validation_grid}
280	  \caption{Independent sub-band validation of the joint scattering index. For each
281	  burst the scattering time $\tau$ is fit directly in four CHIME (blue) and three
282	  DSA-110 (red) sub-bands with $\tau$ free at every frequency, and compared to the
283	  joint-fit power law $\tau_{1\,\mathrm{GHz}}\,\nu^{-\alpha}$ (black); each panel
284	  lists the joint $\alpha$ and the slope $\alpha$ of the independent sub-band points.
285	  \emph{Top:} three well-constrained sightlines whose sub-band $\tau(\nu)$ recover
286	  the joint index to within $1.6\sigma$. \emph{Bottom:} two sightlines with a mild
287	  ($\Delta\alpha\approx0.4$) offset traceable to the intrinsic-width--scattering
288	  degeneracy in a single bright band (FRB~20230325A) and to a low DSA $\tau$
289	  (FRB~20230307A), and one strongly-scattered sightline (FRB~20220506D) whose CHIME
290	  tail is unresolved, leaving only DSA detections over too short a lever to constrain
291	  $\alpha$. Downward triangles are $2\sigma$ upper limits; sub-bands where the
292	  scattered-pulse model does not describe the profile are dropped rather than shown
293	  as limits. Generated by the \texttt{dsa110-FLITS} pipeline.}
294	  \label{fig:subband_tau}
295	\end{figure*}
296	
297	\subsubsection{Gain-marginalized likelihood}
298	\label{sec:gainmarg}
299	
300	The per-channel amplitude of an FRB spectrum is unknown and strongly modulated
301	by scintillation; carried as a free spectral envelope it leaves correlated
302	structure in the fit residuals and invalidates a $\chi^2$ goodness-of-fit test.
303	We therefore marginalize the per-channel gain analytically. With a Gaussian
304	prior on the gain the per-channel evidence is available in closed form (a
305	matched-filter likelihood), and the recovered gain absorbs both the burst
306	spectrum and the scintillation pattern. This removes the spectral and
307	scintillation structure from the residual, so that the reduced $\chi^2$ and the
308	lag-1 residual autocorrelation become meaningful quality gates, and it lowers
309	the sampled dimensionality. We
310	sample the posterior by nested sampling \citep{Skilling2004}, as implemented in
311	\texttt{dynesty} \citep{Speagle2020}, and quality-gate each fit on its reduced
312	$\chi^2$ and residual whiteness, treating $R^2$ and residual normality as
313	informational.
314	
315	\subsubsection{Multiple temporal components}
316	\label{sec:multicomp}
317	
318	Some bursts comprise more than one temporal sub-component, a common feature of
319	the resolved FRB population \citep{Hessels2019, Pleunis2021}. An unmodeled
320	second component is partially absorbed by a single scattered pulse---as a steeper slope
321	and a larger scattering time---and so biases the inferred $\alpha$. We extend
322	the likelihood to $N$ temporal components per band, each contributing its own
323	$(t_0,\zeta)$ while $(\tau_{1\,\mathrm{GHz}},\alpha)$ remain shared across both
324	bands and all components, and we select the number of components per band by
325	Bayesian evidence. Because the evidence normalization differs between likelihood
326	variants, the component counts are compared on a single matched grid in which
327	even the one-component model is evaluated through the multi-component
328	likelihood, so that evidence differences are meaningful. Component arrival times
329	within a band are ordered and separated by a data-derived minimum spacing,
330	keeping the fit identifiable at every prior sample. A clean single-component
331	co-detection in our sample, for which the joint fit returns
332	$\alpha=4.48\pm0.04$ with white residuals, validates the method; a
333	multi-component case is presented in Section~\ref{sec:results-alpha}.
334	% Validating clean case = burst internally labelled "FRB 20230325A" (swap to TNS name).
335	
```

1	\section{Observations and Sample}
2	\label{sec:obs}
3	
4	Our sample is the twelve fast radio bursts detected simultaneously by CHIME/FRB
5	\citep{CHIMEFRB2018} at $0.4$--$0.8$\,GHz and by DSA-110 \citep{Law2024} at
6	$\sim$1.4\,GHz between 2022 February and 2024 February (MJD $59617$--$60369$).
7	The defining asset of the sample is that each burst is seen twice across a
8	$\sim$1\,GHz frequency baseline, which---as developed in
9	Section~\ref{sec:jointfit}---breaks the degeneracy between the scattering time
10	and its spectral index that defeats any single-band fit. The bursts span a
11	dispersion measure range of $262$--$960$\,pc\,cm$^{-3}$; their nicknames, TNS
12	designations, epochs, widths, and time-of-arrival residuals are listed in
13	Table~\ref{tab:toa}. We report all twelve, noting per sightline the level at
14	which each quantity is constrained rather than imposing a single detection
15	threshold.
16	
17	\subsection{Dynamic spectra and reduction}
18	\label{sec:data}
19	
20	For each co-detection we analyze a pair of total-intensity dynamic spectra---one
21	per facility---derived from the CHIME/FRB and DSA-110 baseband recordings
22	(24 spectra in total; provenance and array geometry in
23	\texttt{DATA\_SOURCES.md} of the companion \texttt{dsa110-FLITS} archive). Each
24	spectrum is loaded with its frequency axis standardized to ascending order and
25	its per-channel bandpass normalized, so the fitted amplitudes are free of the
26	band-edge rolloff. All fits, quality gates, and figures in this paper are
27	produced by the open \texttt{dsa110-FLITS} pipeline (\texttt{pipeline/}), which
28	we reference throughout for reproducibility. We do not catalog the fine
29	baseband morphology of each burst here; the only morphological distinction the
30	analysis depends on---whether a burst resolves into more than one temporal
31	component---is treated where it matters, in the multi-component fit of
32	Section~\ref{sec:multicomp}.
33	
34	\subsection{DSA-110 scattering fits}
35	\label{sec:obs-scatt}
36	
37	The joint CHIME--DSA fit yields a $1.4$\,GHz pulse-broadening time
38	$\tau_{1\,\mathrm{GHz}}$ wherever a scattering tail is resolved, measured against
39	the NE2001 Galactic prediction along the sightline. We fit the joint two-band
40	model across the co-detected sample under a single physical (exponential)
41	pulse-broadening function, modeling temporal multiplicity per band
42	(Sections~\ref{sec:results-alpha}, \ref{sec:disc-alpha}). The well-constrained
43	single-screen sightlines return a shared $(\tau_{1\,\mathrm{GHz}},\alpha)$ stable
44	to $\le0.1$ in $\alpha$ against the pulse-broadening-function and multiplicity
45	choices; the marginal sightlines---those whose posterior rails or reaches a prior
46	bound---we treat as unconstrained and quote no index rather than report an
47	artifact of the prior. The final per-sightline $(\tau_{1\,\mathrm{GHz}},\alpha)$
48	from this uniform re-fit is reported in Table~\ref{tab:alpha} (eight
49	well-constrained sightlines); the predicted intervening contribution along every
50	sightline---the quantity we can compute without a measurement---is presented in
51	Section~\ref{sec:budget} and Figure~\ref{fig:budget}, and overlaying the measured
52	$\tau_{1\,\mathrm{GHz}}$ on that prediction is the principal remaining step for the
53	sample.
54	
55	\subsection{Foreground-galaxy search}
56	\label{sec:obs-fg}
57	
58	To establish which sightlines could carry an intervening dispersion or
59	scattering contribution, we searched each of the twelve fields for foreground
60	galaxies and clusters, cross-matching candidates against the DESI Legacy Imaging
61	Survey DR9 with \citet{Zhou2021} photometric redshifts, DESI DR1 spectroscopic
62	redshifts, the NASA/IPAC Extragalactic Database, and PS1-STRM \citep{Beck2021}.
63	The search returns 49 candidate systems---34 galaxy halos and 15 clusters---along
64	nine of the twelve sightlines (three fields yield no candidate). For each
65	candidate we record the proper impact parameter (12--280\,kpc for the halos;
66	clusters by $b/R_{500}$) and the best available redshift and its provenance
67	(spectroscopic or photometric); the full catalog is Table~\ref{tab:foreground}.
68	Spectroscopic host redshifts are available for nine of the twelve bursts
69	($z=0.043$--$0.51$), while three (FRB 20230325A, FRB 20240122A, FRB 20230814B) carry only a
70	placeholder redshift and are excluded from any distance-dependent quantity. We measure no host stellar masses; the only masses entering the
71	analysis are the halo-mass proxies of the intervening systems used by the
72	circumgalactic dispersion model (Section~\ref{sec:dm}), whose confidence we flag
73	per system. The verdict logic that classifies each candidate as foreground,
74	background, or inconclusive, and the resulting dispersion budget, follow in
75	Section~\ref{sec:budget}.
76

=== generate_galaxy_plots.main(): what it reads ===
def main():
    # Resolve paths relative to the repo root
    base_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))
    results_dir = os.path.join(base_dir, "results")
    docs_dir = os.path.join(base_dir, "docs")

    # Import targets from config
    sys.path.insert(0, base_dir)
    from galaxies.v2_0.config import TARGETS
    from galaxies.v2_0.plotting import _split_galaxies_clusters

    target_sections = []

    for name, ra_str, dec_str, z_frb in TARGETS:
        csv_path = os.path.join(results_dir, f"{name.lower()}_galaxies.csv")
        if not os.path.exists(csv_path):
            print(f"  {name}: no CSV found, skipping.")
            continue

        df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)
        # Drop cluster rows: this NFW/L*-halo dashboard models galaxies only; clusters
        # would mis-render as L* halos. Clusters now live in the on-sky maps (plotting.py).
        df, _clusters = _split_galaxies_clusters(df)
        if df.empty:
            print(f"  {name}: no foreground galaxies (clusters shown in sky maps), skipping.")
            continue

        # Build per-galaxy data
        gal_rows = []
        for _, row in df.iterrows():
            z_gal = row["z"]
            impact = row["impact_kpc"]

            if "M_star" in row and not np.isnan(row["M_star"]) and row["M_star"] > 0:
                log_mstar = row["M_star"]
                mass_source = "catalog"
            else:
                phot = estimate_logmstar_from_photometry(row, z_gal)
                if phot is not None:
                    log_mstar, mass_source = phot, "photometric"
                else:
                    # No catalog M* and no usable photometry (e.g. DESI grz
                    # without i-band): assume a typical L* galaxy.
                    log_mstar, mass_source = 10.0, "assumed"

            m_halo = estimate_halo_mass(log_mstar)
            r_vir, r_s, c = get_rvir_and_rs(m_halo, z_gal)
            d_com = cosmo.comoving_distance(z_gal).to(u.Mpc).value

            gal_rows.append(
                {
                    "z_gal": z_gal,
                    "d_com": d_com,
                    "impact": impact,
                    "log_mstar": log_mstar,
                    "mass_source": mass_source,
                    "m_halo": m_halo,
                    "r_vir": r_vir,
                    "r_s": r_s,
                    "c": c,
                }
            )

        if not gal_rows:
            continue

        print(f"  {name}: {len(gal_rows)} galaxies")

        # Generate per-target figures
        fig1, b64_sl = make_sightline_fig(name, z_frb, gal_rows)
        sl_path = os.path.join(results_dir, f"{name.lower()}_sightline.png")

=== main.tex body ===
% Faber2026 — CHIME/FRB–DSA-110 co-detected FRBs: dispersion & scattering budget
%
% Manuscript repo (Overleaf-synced). The analysis pipeline that produces the
% numbers and figures here is dsa110-FLITS, pinned as a git submodule under
% pipeline/ (see PIPELINE.md). Overleaf ignores the submodule and compiles
% the .tex / figures at the repo root.
\documentclass[twocolumn]{aastex631}

\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb}
\usepackage{graphicx}
\graphicspath{{figures/}}

\begin{document}

\title{Disentangling the Dispersion and Scattering Budgets of
CHIME/FRB--DSA-110 Co-detected Fast Radio Bursts}

% Author / affiliation block lives in auth.tex for reuse and clean diffs.
\input{auth.tex}

\begin{abstract}
We present a joint dispersion- and scattering-budget analysis of the twelve fast
radio bursts (FRBs) co-detected by CHIME/FRB ($0.4$--$0.8$\,GHz) and DSA-110
($\sim$1.4\,GHz) between 2022 February and 2024 February, spanning
$\mathrm{DM_{obs}}=262$--$960\,\mathrm{pc\,cm^{-3}}$. The $\sim$1\,GHz baseline
between the two detections breaks the degeneracy between the scattering time and
its spectral index that defeats any single-band fit, which we exploit with a
simultaneous two-band fit for a shared sightline pair
$(\tau_{1\,\mathrm{GHz}},\alpha)$. We decompose each observed DM into Milky-Way
(disk and halo), cosmological (Macquart), intervening circumgalactic, and host
contributions, sourcing the intervening term from a foreground-galaxy search that
returns 49 candidate systems along nine sightlines (29 confirmed foreground). An
intervening column is significant only for the three sightlines that pierce a
foreground galaxy's interior, where the host residual turns formally negative and
thereby bounds the circumgalactic model. For the eight sightlines with a
spectroscopic host redshift we measure band-restricted isotropic-equivalent
energies of $5\times10^{38}$--$1\times10^{41}\,\mathrm{erg}$. A
diffractive-scintillation excess of $\sim$7--11$\times$ the NE2025 Galactic floor
on the mid-latitude sightlines, robust across scattering-calibrated
electron-density models, indicates an extragalactic (host or intervening)
scattering screen rather than a Milky-Way origin. Finally, we show that unmodeled
temporal sub-components can bias the single-component scattering index by several
units, and an unphysical band-dependent pulse-broadening function by several
tenths; re-fitting the sample under a single physical pulse-broadening function
with per-band multiplicity, the well-constrained sightlines yield stable indices,
with the final per-sightline adjudication in progress.
\end{abstract}

\keywords{Radio transient sources (2008) --- Interstellar scattering (854)
--- Intergalactic medium (813) --- Circumgalactic medium (1879)}

\input{sections/intro.tex}
\input{sections/observations.tex}
\input{sections/toa.tex}
\input{sections/budget.tex}
\input{sections/results.tex}
\input{sections/discussion.tex}
\input{sections/conclusions.tex}

\bibliographystyle{aasjournal}
\bibliography{bib/refs}

\end{document}
Shell cwd was reset to /Users/jakobfaber/Developer/repos/github.com/jakobtfaber/dsa110-FLITS

1	"""Pure CGM scattering and dispersion predictions from galaxy scalars."""
2	
3	import math
4	
5	import astropy.units as u
6	import numpy as np
7	
8	from .config import COSMO
9	
10	
11	def _is_bad(x: float | None) -> bool:
12	    """Return True for missing, NaN, or non-finite scalar inputs."""
13	    if x is None:
14	        return True
15	    try:
16	        value = float(x)
17	    except (TypeError, ValueError):
18	        return True
19	    return math.isnan(value) or bool(np.isnan(value)) or not math.isfinite(value)
20	
21	
22	def _clamp(value: float, lo: float, hi: float) -> float:
23	    """Clamp a finite scalar to a closed interval."""
24	    return min(hi, max(lo, value))
25	
26	
27	def dm_halo_mnfw(
28	    m_halo_msun: float,
29	    z_gal: float,
30	    impact_kpc: float,
31	    f_hot: float = 0.75,
32	    y0: float = 2.0,
33	) -> float | None:
34	    """Return the observed mNFW hot-halo DM at projected impact parameter."""
35	    if any(_is_bad(x) for x in (m_halo_msun, z_gal, impact_kpc, f_hot, y0)):
36	        return None
37	
38	    m200 = float(m_halo_msun)
39	    z_value = float(z_gal)
40	    b_kpc = float(impact_kpc)
41	    f_hot_value = float(f_hot)
42	    y0_value = float(y0)
43	    if m200 <= 0.0 or z_value < 0.0 or b_kpc < 0.0 or f_hot_value < 0.0 or y0_value < 0.0:
44	        return None
45	
46	    from scipy.integrate import quad
47	
48	    # Prochaska & Zheng 2019 MNRAS 485,648 set R200 from mass enclosing 200
49	    # times the redshift-dependent critical density, matching the mNFW halo
50	    # truncation used for CGM dispersion predictions in Macquart+2020.
51	    rho_crit = COSMO.critical_density(z_value).to(u.Msun / u.kpc**3).value
52	    if rho_crit <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(rho_crit):
53	        return None
54	    r200_kpc = (3.0 * m200 / (4.0 * math.pi * 200.0 * rho_crit)) ** (1.0 / 3.0)
55	    if r200_kpc <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(r200_kpc):
56	        return None
57	    if b_kpc >= r200_kpc:
58	        return 0.0
59	
60	    # Prochaska & Zheng 2019 MNRAS 485,648 use an NFW-like concentration for
61	    # halo gas; this bounded mass-concentration approximation keeps low/high
62	    # mass extrapolations numerically stable without changing the CGM trend.
63	    concentration = 7.7 * (m200 / 1.0e14) ** (-0.11)
64	    concentration = _clamp(concentration, 3.0, 15.0)
65	    rs_kpc = r200_kpc / concentration
66	    if rs_kpc <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(rs_kpc):
67	        return None
68	
69	    def shape_density(r_kpc: float) -> float:
70	        if r_kpc < 0.0:
71	            return 0.0
72	        y = r_kpc / rs_kpc
73	        # Prochaska & Zheng 2019 MNRAS 485,648 and Macquart+2020 Nature
74	        # 581,391 soften the NFW inner cusp by replacing y with y0+y, so the
75	        # column stays finite through the halo center while retaining an outer
76	        # NFW-like decline.
77	        return 1.0 / ((y0_value + y) * (1.0 + y) ** 2)
78	
79	    def radial_mass_integrand(r_kpc: float) -> float:
80	        # The 4*pi*r^2 term is the spherical volume element needed to normalize
81	        # the mNFW profile to the hot baryon mass rather than an arbitrary rho0.
82	        return 4.0 * math.pi * r_kpc**2 * shape_density(r_kpc)
83	
84	    norm_integral, _ = quad(
85	        radial_mass_integrand, 0.0, r200_kpc, epsabs=0.0, epsrel=1e-6, limit=200
86	    )
87	    if norm_integral <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(norm_integral):
88	        return None
89	
90	    # Macquart+2020 Nature 581,391 appendix anchors halo gas to the cosmic
91	    # baryon allotment; f_hot selects the ionized hot phase within that budget.
92	    baryon_fraction = float(COSMO.Ob0 / COSMO.Om0)
93	    gas_mass_msun = f_hot_value * baryon_fraction * m200
94	    if gas_mass_msun < 0.0 or not math.isfinite(gas_mass_msun):
95	        return None
96	    rho0_msun_kpc3 = gas_mass_msun / norm_integral
97	
98	    # Prochaska & Zheng 2019 MNRAS 485,648 adopt mu_e~1.18 for fully ionized
99	    # primordial-composition CGM gas, converting gas mass density to electron
100	    # density through n_e=rho/(mu_e*m_p).
101	    mu_e = 1.18
102	    m_p_g = 1.67262192369e-24
103	    msun_per_kpc3_to_g_per_cm3 = (1.0 * u.Msun / u.kpc**3).to(u.g / u.cm**3).value
104	    rho0_g_cm3 = rho0_msun_kpc3 * msun_per_kpc3_to_g_per_cm3
105	
106	    def ne_cm3(r_kpc: float) -> float:
107	        return rho0_g_cm3 * shape_density(r_kpc) / (mu_e * m_p_g)
108	
109	    l_half_kpc = math.sqrt(max(r200_kpc**2 - b_kpc**2, 0.0))
110	
111	    def los_integrand(l_kpc: float) -> float:
112	        r_kpc = math.hypot(b_kpc, l_kpc)
113	        return ne_cm3(r_kpc)
114	
115	    column_ne_kpc_cm3, _ = quad(
116	        los_integrand, -l_half_kpc, l_half_kpc, epsabs=0.0, epsrel=1e-6, limit=200
117	    )
118	    if not math.isfinite(column_ne_kpc_cm3):
119	        return None
120	
121	    # One kpc of path contributes 1000 pc to the conventional pc cm^-3 DM
122	    # units, and Macquart+2020 Nature 581,391 divides host/halo rest-frame DM
123	    # by (1+z) because dispersion is observed after cosmological dilation.
124	    dm_obs = column_ne_kpc_cm3 * 1000.0 / (1.0 + z_value)
125	    if not math.isfinite(dm_obs):
126	        return None
127	    return float(max(dm_obs, 0.0))
128	
129	
130	def dm_cool(
131	    dm_halo: float, cool_covering_fraction: float, mgii_wr: float | None = None
132	) -> float | None:
133	    """Return a cool-photoionized CGM DM component scaled by coverage."""
134	    if _is_bad(dm_halo) or _is_bad(cool_covering_fraction):
135	        return None
136	
137	    dm_halo_value = float(dm_halo)
138	    fc = float(cool_covering_fraction)
139	    if dm_halo_value < 0.0 or fc < 0.0:
140	        return None
141	    fc = min(fc, 1.0)
142	
143	    # Werk+2014 ApJ 792,8 and Prochaska+2017 motivate a sub-dominant but
144	    # nonzero T~10^4 K photoionized column; k=0.3 is an explicit prior that the
145	    # cool phase is comparable-to-lower than the hot halo electron column.
146	    k_eff = 0.3
147	    if not _is_bad(mgii_wr):
148	        # Lan & Mo 2018 ApJ 866,36 and Anand+2024 use MgII equivalent width as
149	        # a monotonic proxy for cool CGM column, so tanh gives a bounded EW
150	        # boost without letting saturated absorbers dominate the prior.
151	        k_eff *= 1.0 + 0.5 * math.tanh(float(mgii_wr) / 1.0)
152	
153	    dm_value = dm_halo_value * fc * k_eff
154	    if not math.isfinite(dm_value):
155	        return None
156	    return float(max(dm_value, 0.0))
157	
158	
159	def f_tilde_prior(
160	    sfr_msun_yr: float,
161	    metallicity_12logOH: float | None = None,
162	    agn: bool = False,
163	) -> tuple[float, float, float]:
164	    """Return a monotonic prior bracket for the turbulent scattering factor."""
165	    sfr = 0.0 if _is_bad(sfr_msun_yr) else max(float(sfr_msun_yr), 0.0)
166	
167	    # Ocker+2021 ApJ 911,102 and Cordes+2022 use F_tilde as a lumped
168	    # fluctuation factor; 0.1 is a quiescent-CGM order-of-magnitude floor in
169	    # the absence of a calibrated CGM F(SFR) relation.
170	    value = 0.1
171	    # Ocker+2021 ApJ 911,102 links stronger scattering to clumpier turbulent
172	    # ionized gas; log10(1+SFR) gives a transparent monotonic feedback boost
173	    # that is flat at zero SFR.
174	    value *= 1.0 + math.log10(1.0 + sfr)
175	
176	    if not _is_bad(metallicity_12logOH):
177	        # Asplund+2009 ARA&A 47,481 gives solar 12+log(O/H)~8.7; higher
178	        # metallicity is used here as a mild dust/clumping proxy with bounded
179	        # leverage because no direct CGM F-metallicity calibration exists.
180	        metallicity_factor = 1.0 + 0.3 * (float(metallicity_12logOH) - 8.7)
181	        value *= _clamp(metallicity_factor, 0.5, 2.0)
182	
183	    if agn:
184	        # Ocker+2021 ApJ 911,102 and Cordes+2022 associate stronger feedback
185	        # with enhanced turbulent density fluctuations, motivating a simple
186	        # factor-of-two AGN prior boost.
187	        value *= 2.0
188	
189	    value = max(value, 1e-12)
190	    # Ocker+2021 ApJ 911,102 emphasizes dex-level uncertainty in extragalactic
191	    # scattering environments, so a factor-three bracket encodes roughly
192	    # 0.5 dex of prior width around the deterministic central value.
193	    lo = value / 3.0
194	    hi = value * 3.0
195	    return float(value), float(lo), float(hi)
196	
197	
198	def g_scatt(z_lens: float, z_frb: float) -> float:
199	    """Return intervening-screen geometric scattering leverage in Mpc."""
200	    if _is_bad(z_lens) or _is_bad(z_frb):
201	        return 0.0
202	
203	    z_l = float(z_lens)
204	    z_s = float(z_frb)
205	    if z_l <= 0.0 or z_l >= z_s:
206	        return 0.0
207	
208	    # Macquart & Koay 2013 ApJ 776,125 and Ocker+2021 ApJ 911,102 give the
209	    # thin-screen leverage D_L D_LS / D_S, so a screen at the source has zero
210	    # leverage as D_LS approaches zero.
211	    d_l = COSMO.angular_diameter_distance(z_l).to(u.Mpc).value
212	    d_s = COSMO.angular_diameter_distance(z_s).to(u.Mpc).value
213	    d_ls = COSMO.angular_diameter_distance_z1z2(z_l, z_s).to(u.Mpc).value
214	    if d_l <= 0.0 or d_s <= 0.0 or d_ls <= 0.0:
215	        return 0.0
216	
217	    # Macquart & Koay 2013 ApJ 776,125 / Ocker+2021 ApJ 911,102: the intervening
218	    # thin-screen weight is the angular-diameter combination D_L D_LS / D_S (already
219	    # redshift-aware). The full observer-frame redshift dependence is the canonical
220	    # (1+z_l)^-3, applied ONCE in tau_scat_ms; we do NOT add a screen-dilation factor
221	    # here. The earlier extra /(1+z_l)^2 double-counted to (1+z_l)^-5 and
222	    # over-suppressed high-z screens, biasing the relative z-ranking of sightlines.
223	    leverage = d_l * d_ls / d_s
224	    if leverage <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(leverage):
225	        return 0.0
226	    return float(leverage)
227	
228	
229	def tau_scat_ms(
230	    f_tilde: float,
231	    g_scatt_val: float,
232	    dm_l: float,
233	    z_lens: float,
234	    nu_ghz: float = 1.0,
235	) -> float | None:
236	    """Return pulse-broadening timescale from a lens-galaxy screen in ms."""
237	    if any(_is_bad(x) for x in (f_tilde, g_scatt_val, dm_l, z_lens, nu_ghz)):
238	        return None
239	
240	    f_value = float(f_tilde)
241	    g_value = float(g_scatt_val)
242	    dm_value = float(dm_l)
243	    z_value = float(z_lens)
244	    nu_value = float(nu_ghz)
245	    if f_value < 0.0 or g_value < 0.0 or dm_value < 0.0 or z_value < 0.0 or nu_value <= 0.0:
246	        return None
247	
248	    # Cordes & Lazio 2002 astro-ph/0207156, Cordes+2016 ApJ 832,113, and
249	    # Ocker+2021 ApJ 911,102 give tau proportional to SM*nu^-4 with
250	    # SM~F_tilde*DM^2 for a screen; A=1e-6 ms deliberately lumps the NE2001
251	    # SM-to-tau conversion and screen constants as an order-of-magnitude prior.
252	    a_ms = 1.0e-6
253	    # Macquart & Koay 2013 ApJ 776,125 convention for intervening screens uses
254	    # tau_obs=tau_rest*(1+z)^-3 after combining redshifted observing frequency
255	    # with cosmological time dilation, so the geometric weight is divided by
256	    # (1+z_lens)^3 here.
257	    tau = a_ms * f_value * g_value * dm_value**2 * nu_value ** (-4.0) / (1.0 + z_value) ** 3
258	    if not math.isfinite(tau):
259	        return None
260	    return float(max(tau, 0.0))
261	
262	
263	# Cool CGM enhances the turbulent fluctuation parameter F_tilde relative to the
264	# smooth hot halo: the cool phase shatters to ~pc cloudlets (McCourt+2018 MNRAS
265	# 473,5407), and such clumpy media can dominate radio scattering despite a
266	# sub-dominant electron column (Vedantham & Phinney 2019 MNRAS 483,971;
267	# Ocker+2021 ApJ 911,102). F_cool/F_hot is poorly constrained, so this is an
268	# explicit order-of-magnitude prior with a ~1 dex bracket.
269	COOL_CLUMP_BOOST = 10.0
270	COOL_CLUMP_BOOST_LO = 3.0
271	COOL_CLUMP_BOOST_HI = 30.0
272	
273	
274	def tau_scat_two_phase(
275	    f_tilde: float,
276	    g_scatt_val: float,
277	    dm_hot: float,
278	    dm_cool: float,
279	    z_lens: float,
280	    nu_ghz: float = 1.0,
281	    cool_clump_boost: float = COOL_CLUMP_BOOST,
282	) -> float | None:
283	    """Two-phase intervening-screen scattering: smooth hot halo + clumpy cool CGM.
284	
285	    Scattering measures of independent screens add, and tau is proportional to
286	    SM, so the two phases combine linearly:
287	
288	        tau = tau(F_tilde, dm_hot) + tau(F_tilde * cool_clump_boost, dm_cool)
289	
290	    The cool phase carries a smaller electron column (``dm_cool``) but a much
291	    larger fluctuation parameter because it is clumpy on ~pc scales (McCourt+2018;
292	    Vedantham & Phinney 2019; Ocker+2021); ``cool_clump_boost`` = F_cool/F_hot is
293	    an explicit order-of-magnitude prior. A missing/NaN cool column degrades to
294	    the hot-only screen; if neither phase is usable, returns None.
295	    """
296	    hot = tau_scat_ms(f_tilde, g_scatt_val, dm_hot, z_lens, nu_ghz=nu_ghz)
297	
298	    if _is_bad(f_tilde) or _is_bad(cool_clump_boost) or float(cool_clump_boost) < 0.0:
299	        f_tilde_cool: float | None = None
300	    else:
301	        f_tilde_cool = float(f_tilde) * float(cool_clump_boost)
302	    cool = tau_scat_ms(f_tilde_cool, g_scatt_val, dm_cool, z_lens, nu_ghz=nu_ghz)
303	
304	    if hot is None and cool is None:
305	        return None
306	    total = (hot or 0.0) + (cool or 0.0)
307	    if not math.isfinite(total):
308	        return None
309	    return float(max(total, 0.0))
310	
311	
312	def scint_bandwidth_khz(tau_scat_ms: float) -> float | None:
313	    """Return scintillation decorrelation bandwidth from scattering time."""
314	    if _is_bad(tau_scat_ms):
315	        return None
316	
317	    tau_ms = float(tau_scat_ms)
318	    if tau_ms <= 0.0:
319	        return None
320	
321	    # Cordes & Rickett 1998 ApJ 507,846 give nu_dc=C1/(2*pi*tau) with
322	    # C1~1.16 for a uniform Kolmogorov medium, linking pulse broadening to
323	    # decorrelation bandwidth by Fourier reciprocity.
324	    bandwidth_hz = 1.16 / (2.0 * math.pi * (tau_ms * 1.0e-3))
325	    if bandwidth_hz <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(bandwidth_hz):
326	        return None
327	    return float(bandwidth_hz / 1.0e3)
328	
329	
330	def predict_mgii_wr(impact_kpc: float, logmstar: float | None = None) -> float | None:
331	    """Return predicted MgII 2796 rest equivalent width in Angstrom."""
332	    if _is_bad(impact_kpc):
333	        return None
334	
335	    r_kpc = float(impact_kpc)
336	    if r_kpc <= 0.0:
337	        return None
338	
339	    r_break = 50.0
340	    w0 = 0.8
341	    if not _is_bad(logmstar):
342	        # Churchill+2013 ApJ 763,L42 and Anand+2021/2024 find stronger cool
343	        # absorption around more massive systems; this bounded 0.3-dex-per-dex
344	        # scaling keeps the Nielsen+2013 radial normalization recognizable.
345	        mass_factor = 10.0 ** (0.3 * (float(logmstar) - 10.5))
346	        w0 *= _clamp(mass_factor, 0.2, 3.0)
347	
348	    # Nielsen+2013 ApJ 776,114 MAGIICAT and Anand+2021/2024 show MgII W_r
349	    # anti-correlates with projected distance; the broken power law allows a
350	    # steeper inner decline and shallower outer halo tail around 50 kpc.
351	    if r_kpc <= r_break:
352	        wr = w0 * (r_kpc / r_break) ** (-1.7)
353	    else:
354	        wr = w0 * (r_kpc / r_break) ** (-0.6)
355	    if wr <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(wr):
356	        return None
357	    return float(wr)
358	
359	
360	def cool_covering_fraction(
361	    b_over_rvir: float,
362	    logmstar: float,
363	    is_star_forming: bool,
364	    phi_deg: float | None = None,
365	) -> tuple[float, float, float]:
366	    """Return a prior bracket for the cool CGM covering fraction."""
367	    b_scaled = 10.0 if _is_bad(b_over_rvir) else max(float(b_over_rvir), 0.0)
368	
369	    # Lan & Mo 2018 ApJ 866,36 find cool-gas incidence declines with scaled
370	    # radius and is higher for star-forming galaxies; fc0=0.6/0.3 encodes that
371	    # population split while b_scale=0.5 gives an exponential halo falloff.
372	    fc0 = 0.6 if is_star_forming else 0.3
373	    if not _is_bad(logmstar):
374	        # Lan & Mo 2018 ApJ 866,36 show more cool absorption in more massive
375	        # halos on average, so this bounded tilt is deliberately mild.
376	        mass_tilt = 10.0 ** (0.15 * (float(logmstar) - 10.5))
377	        fc0 *= _clamp(mass_tilt, 0.5, 1.5)
378	    fc = fc0 * math.exp(-b_scaled / 0.5)
379	
380	    if is_star_forming and not _is_bad(phi_deg):
381	        # Bordoloi+2011 ApJ 743,10 and Lan & Mo 2018 ApJ 866,36 motivate
382	        # stronger cool covering along the minor axis from outflow-fed gas, so
383	        # sin(phi) peaks at phi=90 deg and leaves the major axis unboosted.
384	        fc *= 1.0 + 0.5 * math.sin(math.radians(float(phi_deg)))
385	
386	    fc = _clamp(fc, 0.0, 1.0)
387	    # Lan & Mo 2018 ApJ 866,36 highlight population and azimuthal scatter, so
388	    # the bracket widens around the central prior and gets extra upper room
389	    # when star-forming azimuth information is available.
390	    lo = _clamp(fc * 0.5, 0.0, 1.0)
391	    hi = _clamp(fc * 1.5 + (0.2 if (is_star_forming and phi_deg is not None) else 0.0), 0.0, 1.0)
392	    fc = _clamp(fc, lo, hi)
393	    return float(fc), float(lo), float(hi)
394	
395	
396	# --- Cluster ICM dispersion (isothermal beta-model) --------------------------
397	# Mohr+1999 ApJ 517,627 / Arnaud 2009: ICM beta ~ 0.6-0.7, core radius
398	# r_c ~ 0.1-0.2 R500. Cavaliere & Fusco-Femiano 1976 A&A 49,137 set the form.
399	CLUSTER_BETA = 0.65
400	CLUSTER_RC_OVER_R500 = 0.15
401	# Eckert+2019 A&A 621,A40 (X-COP) / Vikhlinin+2006: the hot-gas mass fraction
402	# within R500 spans ~0.10-0.13 and is lower at the smaller radii where most of the
403	# projected column is built. We adopt the lower-middle 0.11 (the 0.13 high end
404	# inflates the DM); even so the isothermal beta-model over-predicts the outskirts,
405	# so dm_cluster_beta_model returns an UPPER BOUND (see its docstring).
406	CLUSTER_F_GAS_500 = 0.11
407	# Fully ionized ICM mean molecular weight per electron (primordial-ish).
408	CLUSTER_MU_E = 1.17
409	# R200/R500 = (M200/M500 * 500/200)^(1/3) = (1.3*2.5)^(1/3) = 1.48, consistent with
410	# config.CLUSTER_M500_TO_M200 = 1.3 (typical c500 ~ 1.5). The earlier 1.54 assumed a
411	# different concentration and was inconsistent with the 1.3 mass ratio used for R200.
412	CLUSTER_R200_OVER_R500 = 1.48
413	
414	
415	def r_delta_kpc(m_delta_msun: float, z: float, delta: float) -> float:
416	    """Spherical-overdensity radius R_Delta (kpc): M = (4/3) pi Delta rho_c(z) R^3.
417	
418	    For Delta=200 this equals the R200 used in get_rvir_and_rs
419	    ((G M/(100 H^2))^(1/3)); written via the critical density to match the R200
420	    derivation already in dm_halo_mnfw.
421	    """
422	    if any(_is_bad(x) for x in (m_delta_msun, z)) or float(m_delta_msun) <= 0.0:
423	        return float("nan")
424	    rho_crit = COSMO.critical_density(float(z)).to(u.Msun / u.kpc**3).value
425	    if rho_crit <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(rho_crit):
426	        return float("nan")
427	    radius = (3.0 * float(m_delta_msun) / (4.0 * math.pi * float(delta) * rho_crit)) ** (1.0 / 3.0)
428	    return float(radius)
429	
430	
431	def _beta_mass_integral_kpc3(beta: float, rc_kpc: float, r_max_kpc: float) -> float:
432	    """Shape-only gas-mass integral int_0^Rmax 4 pi r^2 [1+(r/rc)^2]^(-3beta/2) dr (kpc^3)."""
433	    from scipy.integrate import quad
434	
435	    integrand = lambda r: r * r * (1.0 + (r / rc_kpc) ** 2) ** (-1.5 * beta)  # noqa: E731
436	    value, _ = quad(integrand, 0.0, r_max_kpc, epsabs=0.0, epsrel=1e-7, limit=200)
437	    return 4.0 * math.pi * value
438	
439	
440	def _beta_ne0_cm3(
441	    m500_msun: float,
442	    z: float,
443	    r500_kpc: float,
444	    beta: float,
445	    rc_kpc: float,
446	    f_gas: float = CLUSTER_F_GAS_500,
447	) -> float:
448	    """Central electron density (cm^-3) such that M_gas(<R500) = f_gas * M500.
449	
450	    M_gas = mu_e m_p n_e0 * int 4 pi r^2 shape(r) dr, so n_e0 follows from the
451	    shape integral normalized to the catalog gas mass.
452	    """
453	    shape_int_kpc3 = _beta_mass_integral_kpc3(beta, rc_kpc, r500_kpc)
454	    if shape_int_kpc3 <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(shape_int_kpc3):
455	        return float("nan")
456	    m_gas_g = float(f_gas) * float(m500_msun) * u.Msun.to(u.g)
457	    mu_e_mp_g = CLUSTER_MU_E * 1.67262192369e-24
458	    kpc3_to_cm3 = (1.0 * u.kpc**3).to(u.cm**3).value
459	    return m_gas_g / (mu_e_mp_g * shape_int_kpc3 * kpc3_to_cm3)
460	
461	
462	def dm_cluster_beta_model(
463	    m500_msun: float,
464	    z: float,
465	    impact_kpc: float,
466	    r500_kpc: float | None = None,
467	    beta: float = CLUSTER_BETA,
468	    rc_over_r500: float = CLUSTER_RC_OVER_R500,
469	    f_gas: float = CLUSTER_F_GAS_500,
470	    r_trunc_factor: float = CLUSTER_R200_OVER_R500,
471	) -> float:
472	    """Observer-frame ICM dispersion measure (pc cm^-3) through a beta-model cluster.
473	
474	    Isothermal beta-model n_e(r) = n_e0 [1+(r/r_c)^2]^(-3beta/2) (Cavaliere &
475	    Fusco-Femiano 1976), normalized so the hot gas within R500 is f_gas * M500
476	    (Vikhlinin+2006). The column is projected at impact parameter b, truncated at
477	    r_trunc_factor * R500, and divided by (1+z) for the observer frame
478	    (Macquart+2020 convention). Returns 0.0 for b beyond truncation or bad inputs.
479	
480	    UPPER BOUND: the isothermal beta-model keeps the density slope fixed to large
481	    radius, but real clusters steepen beyond ~R500, so this over-predicts the
482	    projected column by ~1.5-2x relative to the resolved anchor -- Lee+2023 give
483	    ~300 and ~110 pc cm^-3 at b ~ 0.4 and 0.8 R200 for the FRB 20190520B foreground
484	    clusters, where this model returns ~1.7-1.9x more. Treat the value as a
485	    conservative ceiling on the cluster DM, not a point estimate; the upgrade path
486	    is a Vikhlinin+2006 steepening term or a per-cluster X-ray n_e(r) profile.
487	    """
488	    if any(_is_bad(x) for x in (m500_msun, z, impact_kpc)) or float(m500_msun) <= 0.0:
489	        return 0.0
490	    if r500_kpc is None or _is_bad(r500_kpc):
491	        r500_kpc = r_delta_kpc(m500_msun, z, 500)
492	    if _is_bad(r500_kpc) or float(r500_kpc) <= 0.0:
493	        return 0.0
494	    r500_kpc = float(r500_kpc)
495	    rc = float(rc_over_r500) * r500_kpc
496	    r_trunc = float(r_trunc_factor) * r500_kpc
497	    b = float(impact_kpc)
498	    if b >= r_trunc:
499	        return 0.0
500	    ne0 = _beta_ne0_cm3(m500_msun, z, r500_kpc, beta, rc, f_gas=f_gas)
501	    if _is_bad(ne0):
502	        return 0.0
503	
504	    from scipy.integrate import quad
505	
506	    l_half = math.sqrt(max(r_trunc**2 - b**2, 0.0))
507	
508	    def los(l_kpc: float) -> float:
509	        r = math.hypot(b, l_kpc)
510	        return ne0 * (1.0 + (r / rc) ** 2) ** (-1.5 * beta)
511	
512	    column_kpc_cm3, _ = quad(los, -l_half, l_half, epsabs=0.0, epsrel=1e-6, limit=200)
513	    if not math.isfinite(column_kpc_cm3):
514	        return 0.0
515	    # 1 kpc of path contributes 1000 pc to the pc cm^-3 DM unit; /(1+z) for the
516	    # observer frame (Macquart+2020).
517	    dm_obs = column_kpc_cm3 * 1000.0 / (1.0 + float(z))
518	    return float(max(dm_obs, 0.0))
519	
```

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""Per-sightline DM and scattering *budget* attribution for the FRB sample.
3	
4	For each FRB sightline this assembles the observed burst features (dispersion
5	measure DM and, where fit, the scattering time tau) and decomposes them into the
6	media most likely responsible:
7	
8	DM budget (pc/cm^3, observer frame):
9	  DM_obs        observed burst DM (from the burst id encoded in its filename)
10	  DM_MW_ISM     Milky-Way disk ISM, NE2001 (pygedm); YMW16 reported alongside
11	  DM_MW_halo    Milky-Way hot halo, constant prior (Yamasaki & Totani 2020)
12	  DM_cosmic     <DM_cosmic>(z) Macquart relation mean (pure astropy)
13	  DM_interv     sum over foreground galaxies of mNFW hot + cool CGM columns
14	  DM_host       residual = DM_obs - the above (host galaxy, observer frame)
15	
16	Scattering budget (ms at 1 GHz):
17	  tau_obs       measured burst scattering (scat_analysis fit, where available)
18	  tau_MW        Galactic scattering, NE2001 (pygedm) -- negligible at |b| here
19	  tau_interv    sum over foreground galaxies of the predicted two-phase screen
20	  tau_host      not directly predictable; the likely residual
21	
22	The attribution verdict compares the predicted intervening contribution to the
23	measurement: a closely-intersecting, midpoint, cool-gas-bearing foreground
24	galaxy whose predicted tau approaches tau_obs is a coherent case that an
25	intervening galaxy scatters the burst. Where tau_interv << tau_obs the
26	scattering must be host/Milky-Way.
27	
28	DM_cosmic, parse_dm_obs, and read_measured_tau_ms are pure. The Galactic model
29	(galactic_dm_tau) is offline but uses the compiled pygedm NE2001/YMW16 models;
30	it is injectable so the test suite stays network- and pygedm-free.
31	"""
32	
33	from __future__ import annotations
34

36	import json

69	# Below this scaled impact the smooth mNFW hot-halo column is extrapolated
70	# through the galaxy interior (not the CGM it was calibrated for) and the DM
71	# blows up; the "capped" intervening DM evaluates the column at this floor so a
72	# physically bounded value can be reported alongside the raw one.
73	INTERIOR_B_OVER_RVIR = 0.1

75	# In this sample z_frb == 1.0 exactly is a placeholder for an unmeasured host
76	# redshift (Freya/Mahi/Johndoeii). Without a real z the Macquart <DM_cosmic> and
77	# the foreground/background split are meaningless, so those budgets are flagged
78	# and the cosmic/host terms are withheld rather than presented as if real.
